# Depth cues, glow and framing: reference vs ours

Private study. Only general rules and numbers are taken from the 20 reference videos: no traced shots, characters or props.
Working folder: `references/depth/` (scripts, data, crops).
Units: blur is a Gaussian sigma in **720p px** (reference 720x1280) unless marked. Our stills are 540x960 and are scaled ×720/540.
To get a blur-disc radius at our 1080x1920 output, use **R(px @1080p) ≈ 3 × sigma720** (a uniform disc has sigma = R/2, and 1080p is ×1.5).

## 0. The gap in one table

| Cue | Reference (539 frames, every 2 s, 20 videos) | Ours (56 test stills, `out/bridge/test`) | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Background DOF | On in about 1/3 of shots. When on, background sigma720 is CU 4.2, MS 4.0, FS 2.7, WS 1.9 | Background sigma720 0.71-0.99. Excess over subject ≤ 0.71 (median 0.48) | **No visible DOF anywhere.** `DepthOfFieldEffect` caps the blur radius at `bokehScale` px (0.9-2.2 px) |
| Foreground blur | 20% of frames have a near foreground object, which is blurred to sigma720 3.7-7.8 | Almost no foreground objects. Tourist heads in b_200 are sharp (0.96) | Missing |
| Rack focus | None inside shots. Whole-frame blur pulses (sigma 3.6-5) of 0.3-1 s at some cuts | None | Transition-level only; see the motion report |
| Aerial perspective | Things at the skyline sit 40% closer to the horizon colour than near things (dA 0.41 vs 0.69). Darkest far pixels are lifted to 15% of the horizon colour | Far things are *further* from the horizon colour than near ones (0.80 vs 0.70). Dark floor 0.02 | **No haze.** `Fog(#cfe3f2, 90, 560)` hardly reaches the town (40-200 m) |
| Sky gradient (day) | Horizon/upper-sky luminance 1.28. Upper sky #5e93d9 (S 0.54), horizon #9fb6e6 (S 0.32) | 1.04. Upper #8cb6e7 (S 0.39), horizon #9abfe8 | Our upper sky is washed out and too flat |
| Sky (golden) | 1.55 (n = 3). Upper #9a7e7f mauve, horizon #ffd18a bright yellow-orange | 1.05. Upper #b5919f, horizon #cfa197 muddy pink | Orange and purple mix too early |
| Glow | Emissive sources only (moon, lamp, fire, sun): e-fold 14-21 px at 720p, +0.23-0.50 of (source − bg) next to the edge. White faces never glow: hot edges are *sharper* (0.38) than normal edges (0.52) | Nothing reaches the threshold. p99.9 luminance is 0.81 and the maximum is 0.85, so the bloom does nothing | Bloom is inert. There are no emissive sources and no pure whites |
| Camera height | Median 0.79 × character height. 82% eye level, 11% high, 3% low, 3% top-down | Median 0.80 × (1.6 m / 2.0 m), but 29% of shots are below 0.5 × | Slightly too many low shots |
| Headroom / horizon | Head top at y = 0.28 (CU), 0.35 (MS), 0.38 (FS), 0.50 (WS). Horizon at 0.45 | 0.33-0.41. Horizon 0.43-0.48 | OK |
| FOV | Not reliably measurable (see 4.4) | 34-46°, median 40 | No evidence to change |

Comparison sheets (reference on top, ours below):
- `depth/crops/sbs_dof.jpg`: DOF.
- `depth/crops/sbs_haze.jpg`: sky and haze, with swatches for upper sky, sky just above the skyline, and the colour of the first band below the skyline.
- `depth/crops/glow_sources_ref.jpg`: glow around the moon, a lamp, a fireball and the sun.
- `depth/crops/boxes_ref_1..6.jpg` and `boxes_ours_1..3.jpg`: every measured box with its sigma.
- `depth/crops/r04_t6_blurmap.jpg` and `blurviz_A.jpg`: per-edge blur maps.
- `depth/crops/z_*.png`: 2x zooms of blurred reference backgrounds and foregrounds.
- `depth/crops/rack_strip.jpg`: the rack-focus candidates.

---

## 1. Depth of field

### 1.1 Method
- **Edge-spread fit.** At every strong edge, a profile across the edge is fitted with `a + b·Φ((t−t0)/σ) + c·t` using batched Levenberg-Marquardt.
  - Fits are kept only if the relative RMS ≤ 0.07.
  - Detection runs at scales 1, 2 and 4 so that very soft edges are still found.
  - The method was validated on synthetic edges with sigma 0.6-16.
- **Caption mask.** The caption band (white word with a black outline, 55-86% of frame height) is masked out before any statistics.
- **Frame statistics.** Per frame, 40 px tiles take the 30th percentile of their edges. "Above head" is the median tile value above the labelled head top. Shot labels (type, head top/feet, horizon, camera height, foreground, background distance, dutch) were assigned by hand for all 539 frames (`data/labels.csv`).
- **Curated boxes.** On 47 reference frames and 22 of our stills, subject (S), background (B) and foreground (F) boxes were measured with dense sampling (`data/box_stats_ref.csv`, `data/box_stats_ours.csv`).

Scripts: `scripts/blurlib.py`, `batch_blur.py`, `analyze_ref.py`, `box_measure.py`.

### 1.2 How often and how strong (all 530 non-transition frames)

| Type | n | Share with DOF on (bg sigma720 > 1.2) | bg sigma720 when on, median (IQR) | R @1080p | Share > 1.0 overall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CU | 12 | 42% | 4.2 (2.8-4.4) | ~12.5 px | 58% |
| MS | 121 | 21% | 4.0 (1.9-4.9) | ~12 px | 29% |
| FS | 131 | 18% | 2.7 (2.0-3.4) | ~8 px | 23% |
| WS | 55 | 24% | 1.9 (1.4-2.5) | ~5.7 px | 25% |
| Inserts (tile p80) | 117 | 46% | 2.8 | ~8.3 px | — |
| **All** | 530 | — | above-head median 0.57 (IQR 0.38-1.07, p90 2.75) | — | 27% |

**Sharp baseline.** The 10th percentile of edge sigma per frame is 0.33 (IQR 0.30-0.37). Reference line work is crisp, so blur is a deliberate per-shot choice.

**Per video.** The above-head median ranges from 0.38 (videos 09 and 10, 0-5% of frames blurred) to 2.12 (video 04, 64% blurred).

**DOF is not a fixed lens.** In FS with a far background, the median is only 0.62 (20% > 1.0, 0% > 2.0). In MS with a near wall it is 0.60 (30% > 1.0). DOF is switched on for chosen beats, mostly intimate CU/MS moments, inserts and foreground-framed shots, and switched off for establishing and most full shots.

### 1.3 Measured examples (sigma720, p30 of each box)

| Frame | Type | Subject | Background | Foreground |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 05_f2010 | MS | 0.30 | 6.97 (photo wall behind) | — |
| 13_f0930 | CU | 0.34 | 5.33 (forest, also hazed) | — |
| 05_f2250 | MS | — | 4.92 | — |
| 08_f0210 | MS over-the-shoulder | 0.36 | 4.53 | 3.71 (head in front) |
| 11_f0150 / 03_f0810 | MS | sharp | 3.16 / 2.76 | — |
| 03_f0930 / 04_f0210 / 04_f0390 / 04_f0330 | FS | ~0.35 | 2.80 / 2.54 / 2.35 / 1.85-2.74 | ground 10.8 (04_f0210) |
| 17_f0270 | MS | 0.44 | 1.77 | **7.79** (slot machines in front) |
| 16_f0990 | MS | 0.29 | 0.32 (sharp booth) | **5.52** (cat in front) |
| 14_f0030 / 01_f0210 / 07_f0750 | FS | sharp | 0.99 / 0.96 / 0.75 | — |
| 16_f0150 / 14_f0390 | WS | sharp | 1.27 / 0.74 | — |
| 10_f1650 | CU | 3.26 | 3.42 | focus on a foreground key |
| 01_f0570 | FS | 1.81 (near character) | focus on the far ship | — |

Ours (all 21 stills with S+B):
- Subject 0.60-1.02 (median 0.76).
- Background 0.71-0.99 (median 0.89).
- Excess background blur sqrt(B² − S²) has a median of 0.48 and a maximum of 0.71.
- Only foreground: b_200 tourist heads at 0.96.
- Our stills are half resolution, so their absolute sharpness is not comparable. The B−S difference is the fair measure, and it is about zero.

### 1.4 How blur grows behind the subject
- **Thin-lens shape with saturation.** CoC(d) = C∞·|1 − F/d| fits the reference.
  - Crowd 03_f1830: heads of diameter 200 → 135 → 110 → 70 px (distance ratio 1 → 1.5 → 1.8 → 2.9) have sigma 0.33 → 0.33 → 1.14-1.31 → 1.55.
  - Thin lens with C∞ = 2.4 sigma predicts 0, 0.8, 1.07 and 1.58.
- **Saturation.** In the western street (04 at 6 s), every building from mid to far distance sits at sigma 1.85-2.0. Blur reaches its limit within about 3× the focus distance and then stays flat.
- **Near walls.** A wall about 1 m behind a character is blurred much less than the sky or town in the same kind of shot. This follows from thin-lens geometry and does not need a separate rule.

### 1.5 Rack focus
Three candidate windows were checked frame by frame (`scripts/rack.py`, `crops/rack_strip.jpg`, 6 horizontal bands per frame):
- 04 frames 100-220.
- 10 frames 1600-1700.
- 01 frames 540-620.

**No focus pulls inside shots were found.**

- In 04, a new shot starts with the whole frame at sigma 3.6 for 0.8 s, then 1.8 for 1.1 s, then a whip pan.
- In 10, a push-in blurs the whole frame to sigma 4-5 for 0.8 s, then cuts to sharp.

These are transition blurs (motion report), not DOF.

### 1.6 Why ours shows nothing (code)
postprocessing 6.37.2 `DepthOfFieldEffect` works like this:
- CoC = `smoothstep(0, focusRange, |d − F|)` in world units.
- Bokeh is a 64-tap Vogel disc of radius `coc × bokehScale × texelSize`, and the bokeh materials are sized to the **full** 1080x1920 buffer.
- So the maximum disc radius is `bokehScale` px.
- Our 2.2 / 1.5 / 0.9 px gives sigma720 ≈ 0.7 / 0.5 / 0.3, which is below the baseline.

Raising `bokehScale` to the 8-20 px we need has three problems:
- 64 taps spread over a 20 px disc look grainy.
- The far buffer is premultiplied by CoC without renormalising, which risks dark halos around the subject.
- The smoothstep CoC under-blurs foreground objects. At d = F/2 it gives 0.16 where the thin lens gives 1.0.

**Replace it with a thin-lens gather effect (recipe 6.1).**

---

## 2. Atmosphere

### 2.1 Method
`scripts/atmos.py` writes `data/atmos_ref.csv` and `data/atmos_ours.csv`.

- **Sky mask.** The sky is the smooth (|∇L| < 0.01/px), bright (L > 0.35) region connected to the top edge.
- **Skyline.** The median of the lowest sky pixel per column.
- **Sky colours.**
  - "Upper" is the median colour of the top 15% of the visible sky.
  - "Low" is the band just above the skyline (the airlight A).
- **Haze below the skyline**, in 5 bands (0-4, 4-10, 10-20, 20-35, 35-100% of frame height below it):
  - **Dark floor:** p10 of the normalised dark channel minfilter(min_c I_c/A_c). 0 means full-strength ink or shadow; 1 means everything is lifted to the horizon colour.
  - **dA:** the RGB distance from the band's median colour to A.
- Reference frames are restricted to the 40 exteriors with a labelled horizon. All 43 of our stills that contain sky are used.

### 2.2 Results

| Metric | Ref day (n 37) | Ours day (n 30) | Ref golden (n 3) | Ours golden (n 13) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Upper sky | **#5e93d9**, L 0.53, S 0.54 | #8cb6e7, L 0.69, S 0.39 | **#9a7e7f**, L 0.55 | #b5919f, L 0.60 |
| Sky above skyline | **#9fb6e6**, L 0.72, S 0.32 | #9abfe8, L 0.73, S 0.34 | **#ffd18a**, L 0.84 | #cfa197, L 0.67 |
| Low/upper luminance | **1.28** (1.18-1.40) | 1.04 (1.01-1.05) | **1.55** (1.49-1.87) | 1.05 |
| Dark floor just below skyline | 0.15 (0-0.24) | 0.02 | 0.12 | 0.00 |
| dA: far band → near band | **0.41 → 0.69** | 0.80 → 0.70 | 0.60 → 0.78 | 0.77 → 0.82 |
| Band colour just below skyline | #b2b0a1 (pale grey-beige) | #7c4d4b (dark brick red) | #be755f | #87270b |

### 2.3 What this means
- **Day, reference.** Skyline objects are blended about 40% toward the horizon colour (k ≈ 1 − 0.41/0.69). Their ink and shadows lift by about 15%. Near objects keep full colour.
- **Ours.** Our skyline town (40-180 m away) is darker and more saturated than the foreground.
- **Why our fog does nothing.** Fog 90 → 560 is 0 at 70 m, 0.085 at 130 m and 0.19 at 180 m. Its colour #cfe3f2 is also a pale cyan that does not match our horizon.
- **Sky gradient.** The reference keeps a pale horizon and reaches a deep, saturated blue within about 20° elevation. Our shader `mix(hor, top, pow(h, 0.55))` (h = sin(elevation)) hits about 50% at the top of a 40° frame, so the whole visible sky looks the same pale blue.
- **Golden.** Our sky is muddy because the orange horizon is already 30-40% mixed with the purple-blue top at the skyline.

---

## 3. Bloom and glow

### 3.1 Method
`scripts/bloom.py`, `scripts/glow_crops.py`, `scripts/sim_bloom.py`.

**(a) Edge widths.** ESF sigma of edges whose bright side is hot (local max L ≥ 0.95) vs normal edges (0.45-0.85), per frame.
- Reference: hot 0.38, normal 0.52. Per frame hot/normal = 0.80; only 2% of frames are above 1.5.
- **White surfaces (heads, eyes, walls, white clouds) do not bloom.** Their edges are the crispest in the frame.
- Ours: 1.01. No hot pixels exist at all.

**(b) Emissive sources** (`crops/glow_sources_ref.jpg`). The radial profile from the centroid of the L ≥ 0.85 blob. "Excess" = (L − bg)/(L_src − bg).

| Source | Disc r0 (px720) | Excess at r0+4 | r0+10 | r0+20 | r0+40 | e-fold (px720) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Moon, 11_f1350 | 38 | 0.37 | 0.32 | 0.22 | 0.10 | 19.6 |
| Moon, 11_f1050 | 40 | 0.23 | 0.21 | 0.15 | 0.05 | 18.0 |
| Ceiling lamp, 05_f0210 | 42 | 0.46 | 0.38 | 0.30 | 0.14 | 20.9 |
| Fireball, 12_f0930 | 36 | 0.42 | 0.23 | — | 0.05 | 14.4 |
| Sun, 11_f0930 | 58 | 0.50 | 0.49 | 0.41 | 0.11 | 19.7 |

**Target glow.** A broad, flat halo:
- excess 0.25-0.5 right at the edge;
- still 0.15-0.4 at +20 px (720p);
- e-fold 14-21 px at 720p, which is **21-31 px at 1080p** (about 1.5% of frame height).
- The sun scene washes 7% of the frame to L ≥ 0.93.

**Peak white.**
- Reference: p99.9 luminance 0.99, and the maximum is 1.0 in nearly every frame.
- Ours: p99.9 0.81, maximum 0.85 (p90 of frames: 0.97).
- Nothing in our frames crosses `luminanceThreshold 0.86`. This is a grade/exposure issue for the colour report, but it is the reason our bloom is invisible.

### 3.2 Calibrating the mipmap bloom (numpy port of MipmapBlurPass)
The port uses the same 13-tap downsample (inner 4 at 0.125, outer 9 at 1/18), the 9-tap tent upsample, and `mix(down[i], up, radius)`. Output is at half resolution. g(d) is the glow d px (1080p) outside a 60 px source of unit masked value.

| levels | radius | g(4) | g(20) | g(60) | g(150) | e-fold (px1080) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 (our current) | 0.85 | 0.14 | 0.049 | 0.017 | 0.0067 | 40 (long thin tail) |
| 6 | 0.5 | 0.13 | 0.022 | 0.004 | 0.0004 | 24.5 |
| **6** | **0.6** | ~0.15 | ~0.04 | ~0.011 | ~0.001 | **~30** |
| 6 | 0.7 | 0.18 | 0.057 | 0.019 | 0.002 | 35 |
| 5 | 0.85 | 0.26 | 0.124 | 0.022 | 0.00001 | 21 |

- **levels 6, radius 0.6** matches the reference e-fold.
- The reference halo is flatter near the source than any mip kernel. For the few big sources, add an additive glow sprite (recipe 6.4).
- Small sparks (r0 6 px) get only g(4) ≈ 0.045, so they need a much higher HDR value.

---

## 4. Composition

From `data/labels.csv` (530 frames) and our CAMS (`story.ts`, character card height 2.0-2.1 m with `SU = 0.00216`).

### 4.1 Shot mix and framing (reference)

| Type | Share | Head-top y (IQR) | Visible character height | Feet y | Foreground object |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CU | 3% | 0.28 (0.21-0.35) | 0.65 | cut | 39% |
| MS | 27% | 0.35 (0.25-0.38) | 0.50 | cut at ~0.80 | 38% (s 46, b 9) |
| FS | 28% | 0.38 (0.35-0.42) | 0.35 (0.27-0.45) | 0.75 | 16% |
| WS | 12% | 0.50 (0.42-0.55) | 0.15 | 0.64 | 16% |
| EWS | 8% | 0.51 | 0.10 | 0.59 | 5% |
| Inserts | 22% | — | — | — | 9% |

Other reference framing numbers:
- **Horizon** (FS/WS/EWS): y = 0.45 (IQR 0.39-0.50).
- **Background distance:** near wall 58%, mid 23%, far 20%.
- **Dutch tilt:** 2% (11 of 530).
- **Placement:** characters sit in the middle of the vertical frame, with the caption over their lower body.

### 4.2 Camera height
**Reference.**
- Labels: eye level 82%, high 11%, low 3.4%, top-down 3.2%.
- From horizon geometry (34 FS/WS/EWS frames, level camera): camera height = (feet − horizon)/(feet − head) = **0.79 × character height**, IQR 0.67-1.00.
- 10% of these frames are below 0.5×. High shots run 1.5-1.9×.

**Ours (CAMS).**
- Height above the deck: median 1.6 m = **0.80 ×** (matches).
- But 29% of shots are below 0.5× (≤ 1.0 m):
  - f48: 0.6 m
  - f88: 0.4 m
  - f353 and f440: 0.36 m
  - f823: 0.7 m
  - f1685: 0.8 m
- 47% are at 1.0-2.2 m. The rest are high shots and aerials.

### 4.3 Ours vs targets (from CAMS geometry; feet and head projected for a character at the look point)
- **Head top.** Our MS shots (f585/640/925/984) have the head top at 0.35-0.40 and a full-height equivalent of 0.74-0.77, which is the same as the reference MS. Our FS shots (f283, f768, f1729, f1857) have the head top at 0.29-0.41 and character height 0.32-0.41, matching the reference FS.
- **Horizon** 0.42-0.49 on level shots (matches).
- **Foreground objects:** essentially none (reference: 38% of MS and CU).
- **Inserts:** we have about 3 (anchor, snap, boots) vs 22% in the reference. This is out of scope here, but those shots carry most of the reference's DOF.

### 4.4 FOV
- I tried vanishing points: straight segments, RANSAC, then f² = −(v1−c)·(v2−c).
- It failed validation on our own stills: on c_1800 it estimated 25° against a true 42°.
- On reference frames it gave inconsistent values (1-73°), because stylised architecture is rarely orthogonal and many segments sit on frame edges.
- The ground-plane cues (horizon vs feet vs character size) cancel the focal length.
- Character sizes and positions at our distances already match the reference at fov 36-44.
- **Recommendation: keep fov 34-46; there is no measurable reason to change it.**

---

## 5. Gaps ranked by visual impact (top 5)

1. **DOF on chosen shots.**
   - Ours: zero. Reference: about 1/3 of shots.
   - Use a thin-lens gather effect: C∞ 12-14 px (CU/MS/inserts), 8 (FS when used), 5 (WS when used); foreground 15-24 px.
   - Focus on the subject's eyes.
2. **Aerial perspective.**
   - Fog colour = horizon colour; linear fog 25 → 420 m (day) and 20 → 360 m (golden).
   - The skyline town is then pulled 20-40% toward the horizon, and the ink is fog-tinted.
   - This takes our skyline from #7c4d4b-dark to the reference's pale grey-beige.
3. **Sky gradient.**
   - Deep upper blue #5e93d9 at about 20° elevation, pale horizon (luminance ratio 1.28).
   - Golden: bright #ffd08a horizon that holds to about 3°, then mauve/indigo.
4. **Foreground framing objects in about 1/3 of MS/CU.**
   - Rail post, chain link, flag, a spectator's shoulder or hat, at 0.3-0.5 × the focus distance and covering 10-25% of a frame edge.
   - The new DOF blurs them to the reference's 4-8 sigma720.
5. **Glow only on emissive sources and real whites.**
   - Bloom: threshold 1.0, levels 6, radius 0.6, intensity 0.5.
   - Emissive HDR ×3 (sun, lamps, fire), ×8 for sparks.
   - Faces stay crisp.
   - Pair with the colour report's exposure fix so white heads and clouds reach 0.97-1.0.
   - Minor: raise f48 and f823 to about 1.4 m so fewer than 15% of shots are low-angle.

---

## 6. Recipe (our stack: three 0.175, postprocessing 6.37.2, @react-three/postprocessing 3.0.4)

### 6.1 New file `src/videos/bridge/lensblur.ts`: thin-lens DOF, single gather pass
- `@react-three/postprocessing` 3.0.4 puts any `CONVOLUTION` effect into its own `EffectPass` automatically, so `<primitive object={fx.lens} />` can replace `fx.dof` in place (after ink, before bloom).
- `getViewZ`, `readDepth`, `texelSize` and `inputBuffer` are provided by `EffectPass`, as `ink.ts` already relies on.

```ts
// Thin-lens depth of field in one gather pass (scatter-as-gather), for the reference's soft backgrounds.
// CoC radius in output px = cInf * |1 - focus / depth| (near side x nearScale), clamped to maxCoC.
// Pass 1 gathers the pixel's own disc; samples farther than the pixel may not spread over it (keeps the subject's
// silhouette clean). Pass 2 lets a blurred foreground spread over sharper pixels behind it.
import { Effect, EffectAttribute, BlendFunction } from "postprocessing";
import { Uniform } from "three";

const frag = /* glsl */ `
uniform float focus;      // focus distance along the view axis (m)
uniform float cInf;       // blur-disc radius (px, output res) of things at infinity; 0 = off
uniform float nearScale;  // extra blur for things in front of the focus plane
uniform float maxCoC;     // px
#define TAPS 64
#define NEAR_TAPS 32
#define GOLDEN 2.39996323

float cocAt(float d) {
  float c = cInf * (1.0 - focus / max(d, 1e-3));
  if (c < 0.0) c *= nearScale;
  return clamp(c, -maxCoC, maxCoC);
}
float distAt(vec2 uv) { return -getViewZ(readDepth(uv)); }
float hash12(vec2 p) { return fract(sin(dot(p, vec2(12.9898, 78.233))) * 43758.5453); }

void mainImage(const in vec4 inputColor, const in vec2 uv, const in float depth, out vec4 outputColor) {
  if (cInf < 0.25) { outputColor = inputColor; return; }
  float d0 = -getViewZ(depth);
  float r0 = abs(cocAt(d0));
  float rot = hash12(gl_FragCoord.xy) * 6.2831853;   // fixed per pixel: no frame-to-frame shimmer
  vec3 acc = inputColor.rgb; float wsum = 1.0;
  if (r0 > 0.5) {
    for (int i = 0; i < TAPS; i++) {
      float rr = sqrt((float(i) + 0.5) / float(TAPS)) * r0;
      float a = float(i) * GOLDEN + rot;
      vec2 suv = uv + vec2(cos(a), sin(a)) * rr * texelSize;
      float ds = distAt(suv);
      float rs = abs(cocAt(ds));
      if (ds > d0) rs = min(rs, r0);                // background never bleeds over a nearer pixel
      float w = clamp(rs - rr + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);      // does the sample's disc reach us?
      acc += texture2D(inputBuffer, suv).rgb * w; wsum += w;
    }
  }
  vec3 col = acc / wsum;
  vec3 fg = vec3(0.0); float fw = 0.0, cov = 0.0;
  for (int i = 0; i < NEAR_TAPS; i++) {
    float rr = sqrt((float(i) + 0.5) / float(NEAR_TAPS)) * maxCoC;
    if (rr <= r0) continue;                          // already covered by pass 1
    float a = float(i) * GOLDEN + rot + 1.0;
    vec2 suv = uv + vec2(cos(a), sin(a)) * rr * texelSize;
    float ds = distAt(suv);
    if (ds >= d0 * 0.97) continue;                   // only things in front of us
    float rs = abs(cocAt(ds));
    float w = clamp(rs - rr + 0.5, 0.0, 1.0);
    fg += texture2D(inputBuffer, suv).rgb * w; fw += w;
    cov += w * (maxCoC * maxCoC) / max(rs * rs, 1.0);
  }
  if (fw > 0.0) col = mix(col, fg / fw, clamp(cov / float(NEAR_TAPS), 0.0, 1.0));
  outputColor = vec4(col, inputColor.a);
}`;

export class LensBlurEffect extends Effect {
  constructor({ maxCoC = 24, nearScale = 1.2 } = {}) {
    super("LensBlurEffect", frag, {
      blendFunction: BlendFunction.NORMAL,
      attributes: EffectAttribute.DEPTH | EffectAttribute.CONVOLUTION,
      uniforms: new Map<string, Uniform>([
        ["focus", new Uniform(10)], ["cInf", new Uniform(0)],
        ["nearScale", new Uniform(nearScale)], ["maxCoC", new Uniform(maxCoC)],
      ]),
    });
  }
  set focus(v: number) { this.uniforms.get("focus")!.value = v; }
  set cInf(v: number) { this.uniforms.get("cInf")!.value = v; }
}
```

**Cost.** About 96 taps × 2 fetches per pixel at 1080x1920. This is fine on the ANGLE GPU path.

**Sky.** Depth 1.0 means the far plane (1500 m), so the sky and clouds get the full `cInf`, which matches the soft clouds in reference DOF shots.

**Fallback if you only want a quick test with the built-in effect:**
`new DepthOfFieldEffect(camera, { resolutionScale: 1 })`, `worldFocusRange = 2 * focus`, `bokehScale = cInf`. This approximates the far side of the thin lens but under-blurs foreground objects. Expect grain and dark rims above 8 px.

### 6.2 `Bridge.tsx` (replaces the `dof` lines)

```ts
import { LensBlurEffect } from "./lensblur";
import { dofAt } from "./story";
const fwd = new THREE.Vector3(), fpt = new THREE.Vector3();   // module scope

const fx = useMemo(() => ({
  ink: new InkEffect(),
  lens: new LensBlurEffect({ maxCoC: 24, nearScale: 1.2 }),
  bloom: new BloomEffect({ intensity: 0.5, luminanceThreshold: 1.0, luminanceSmoothing: 0.2, mipmapBlur: true, levels: 6, radius: 0.6 }),
  tone: new ToneMappingEffect({ mode: ToneMappingMode.NEUTRAL }),
}), []);
// <primitive object={fx.lens} dispose={null} />  in place of fx.dof

// per frame, after target.copy(world.update(id, f, cam, scene)):
const d = dofAt(f);
cam.getWorldDirection(fwd);
if (d.focus) fpt.set(...d.focus(f)); else fpt.copy(target);
fx.lens.focus = Math.max(0.3, fpt.sub(cam.position).dot(fwd));   // view-axis distance, not Euclidean
fx.lens.cInf = d.cInf;
```

### 6.3 `story.ts`: a DOF table next to CAMS (same `f`/`dur` as the camera entries, so blur ramps with camera moves)

```ts
/** Lens blur per shot (thin lens). cInf = blur-disc radius in px at 1080x1920 for things at infinity; 0 = all sharp.
 *  Reference: DOF on in ~1/3 of shots; when on, background sigma720 CU 4.2, MS 4.0, FS 2.7, WS 1.9,
 *  foreground objects 4-8 (disc radius at 1080p = 3 x sigma720). Establishing/wide/most full shots stay sharp. */
export type Dof = { f: number; dur?: number; cInf: number; focus?: (f: number) => V3 };
const eyes = (id: number) => (f: number): V3 => [0, D0 + 1.6, Fz(id, f)];
const boots = (id: number) => (f: number): V3 => [0, D0 + 0.3, Fz(id, f)];
export const DOF: Dof[] = [
  { f: 0, cInf: 0 },                                   // establishing
  { f: 88, dur: 9, cInf: 12, focus: eyes(1) },          // low MS, front soldier
  { f: 126, cInf: 0 },                                  // aerial wides, FS
  { f: 353, dur: 10, cInf: 16, focus: boots(3) },       // boots CU
  { f: 440, cInf: 16, focus: boots(4) },
  { f: 444, dur: 13, cInf: 0 },                         // side WS / FS
  { f: 534, dur: 13, cInf: 10, focus: eyes(5) },        // MS
  { f: 585, dur: 10, cInf: 12, focus: eyes(5) },
  { f: 640, cInf: 12, focus: eyes(6) },
  { f: 643, dur: 11, cInf: 14 },                        // CU, focus = look (the face)
  { f: 717, dur: 12, cInf: 0 },                         // high WS, FS, low WS
  { f: 871, dur: 11, cInf: 8 },                         // high MS, focus = look
  { f: 925, dur: 11, cInf: 12, focus: eyes(7) },        // MS
  { f: 984, cInf: 12, focus: eyes(8) },
  { f: 987, dur: 12, cInf: 14 },                        // anchor insert, focus = look
  { f: 1039, dur: 8, cInf: 10 },                        // snap insert
  { f: 1062, dur: 18, cInf: 0 },                        // aerial, river, park, bank FS
  { f: 1770, dur: 12, cInf: 10 },                       // officer MS, focus = look
  { f: 1857, cInf: 0 },
  { f: 1905, dur: 10, cInf: 10 },                       // MS
  { f: 1962, dur: 24, cInf: 0 },                        // closing aerial
];
export const dofAt = (f: number) => {
  let i = 0; while (i + 1 < DOF.length && f >= DOF[i + 1].f) i++;
  const d = DOF[i], p = DOF[Math.max(0, i - 1)];
  const k = d.dur ? Math.min(1, (f - d.f) / d.dur) : 1;
  return { cInf: p.cInf + (d.cInf - p.cInf) * k, focus: d.focus };
};
```

**Notes on the DOF table.**
- **Coverage.** These values put DOF on about 35% of screen time: CU/MS beats, inserts and the boots shot. That matches the reference share.
- **Fallback.** If a shot reads too soft, halve `cInf` before turning it off. Reference "on" shots never go below sigma720 1.2 (about 4 px radius).
- **Foreground objects.** For the reference's foreground framing, place an object at 0.3-0.5 × the focus distance in DOF shots. With nearScale 1.2 it lands at 15-24 px, the same as the reference's 4-8 sigma720.

### 6.4 Bloom: glow only where light is emitted
- **Bloom settings.**
  - `luminanceThreshold 1.0`: no lit surface crosses it, so white heads stay crisp as in the reference.
  - `luminanceSmoothing 0.2`, `intensity 0.5`, `mipmapBlur true`, `levels 6`, `radius 0.6`.
  - This gives an e-fold of about 30 px at 1080p, the reference's 14-21 px at 720p.
  - The current `levels 8 / radius 0.85` makes a thin, long, frame-wide veil instead.
- **Emissive sources.**
  - Use HDR colours (the composer buffer is half-float, and r3f-postprocessing turns renderer tone mapping off).
  - Big sources: `new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ color: new THREE.Color(3.0, 2.6, 2.0) })` for the sun disc (golden shots), lanterns and fire.
  - Sparks: `(8, 6, 3)`, for example where the bolt snaps.
  - Expected excess next to a big source: 3 × 0.5 × g(4 px) ≈ 0.22 linear, which is the reference's +0.25-0.5 of (source − bg).
- **Big sources (sun, lamp): add a flat halo sprite** to match the reference's flat glow:
  - additive `Sprite` of radius 2.2 × the source radius;
  - texture alpha = 0.35 × exp(−(r/(1.1·r_src))²);
  - `blending: THREE.AdditiveBlending`, `depthWrite: false`, `fog: false`.
- **Exposure (colour report).** Whites must reach 0.97-1.0, as in the reference (p99.9 0.99). Ours peak at 0.85.

### 6.5 Fog and sky (`world.ts` + the `LIGHTS` table)
**LIGHTS table.**

```ts
// LIGHTS (where top / hor / fog live)
day:    { /* … */ top: "#4284d3", hor: "#c9cdee", skyCurve: [0.0, 0.5, 0.6],  fog: "#b0c0e8", fogNear: 25, fogFar: 420 },
golden: { /* … */ top: "#443876", hor: "#ffd08a", skyCurve: [0.05, 0.6, 1.0], fog: "#f4c68c", fogNear: 20, fogFar: 360 },
```

**Sky shader** (constructor; `h` = sin(elevation)).

```ts
uniforms: { top: { value: new THREE.Color() }, hor: { value: new THREE.Color() }, curve: { value: new THREE.Vector3(0, 0.5, 0.6) } },
fragmentShader: `uniform vec3 top; uniform vec3 hor; uniform vec3 curve; varying vec3 vP;
  void main(){ float h = clamp(vP.y, 0.0, 1.0);
    float k = pow(clamp((h - curve.x) / (curve.y - curve.x), 0.0, 1.0), curve.z);
    gl_FragColor = vec4(mix(hor, top, k), 1.0); }`,
```

**`update()`.**

```ts
this.skyMat.uniforms.curve.value.set(...L.skyCurve);
this.fog.color.set(L.fog); this.fog.near = L.fogNear; this.fog.far = L.fogFar;
```

**Check of the sky values.**
- Day: at 4° elevation the sky is #a0b7e6, and at 20° it is #5e93d9. Both match the reference.
- Golden: it stays #ffd08a up to 3°, and is #9c807f at 20° (reference #9a7e7f).
- NEUTRAL tone mapping compresses linear values above 0.76, so check one still per light.

**What the new fog does.**

| Distance | Fog amount |
|---|---|
| Soldiers (≤ 15 m) | 0 |
| Far tower (~57 m) | 0.08 |
| Far-bank riverfront (70 m) | 0.11 |
| Back rows (100-140 m) | 0.19-0.29 |
| Main street (180 m) | 0.39 |
| Aerial shots of the town (250 m) | 0.57 |

**Fog-tinted ink (`ink.ts`, coordinate with the ink report).** The reference lifts far ink by about 15%. Add `fogColor`, `fogNear` and `fogFar` uniforms and replace `ink` in the final mix:

```glsl
float fogK = clamp((dist - fogNear) / (fogFar - fogNear), 0.0, 1.0);
vec3 inkC = mix(ink, fogColor, fogK * 0.6);
outputColor = vec4(mix(inputColor.rgb, inkC, k), inputColor.a);
```

Also raise `farFade` to about 600, so far outlines haze out instead of vanishing at 260 m.

### 6.6 Composition rules for CAMS
- **Keep:**
  - head top at y ≈ 0.28 (CU), 0.35 (MS), 0.38 (FS), 0.50 (WS);
  - horizon at 0.40-0.50;
  - fov 34-46.
- **Camera height.**
  - Default: 0.7-1.0 × character height = **1.4-2.0 m above the deck** (reference median 0.79×).
  - Keep the deliberately low beats (boots f353/440, f88).
  - Raise f48 (0.6 → 1.4 m) and f823 (0.7 → 1.4 m). That brings low shots (below 0.5×) from 29% to about 15%. The reference has 3% labelled low and 10% below 0.5×.
- **Foreground framing.** In about 1/3 of MS/CU (for example f534, f585, f925, f1770), put a near object at 0.3-0.5 × the focus distance covering 10-25% of a frame edge. Reference: 38% of MS/CU, blurred to sigma720 4-8.
- **Dutch tilt.** The reference uses it in 2% of frames. Keep our rolls ≤ 0.05 rad and use them on at most 2-3 shots.
- **No focus pulls inside shots.** Change DOF only at cuts, or ramp it with camera moves (the `dur` in the table).

---

**Caveats.**
- Our stills are half-resolution JPEG test renders, so compare blur *differences* (background vs subject), not absolute sharpness.
- Golden-hour reference n = 3.
- Bloom calibration is a CPU port of the shader. Verify on one emissive still.
- Nothing was rendered and nothing under `your Remotion project` was edited.


# Lighting: reference vs ours

This is a private study. Numbers come from 67 reference study frames (20 videos, 720x1280, captions masked), 539
frames taken every 2 s for the grade stats, and 36 of our stills (540x960, scaled to 720-wide equivalents where pixel
widths are quoted). Scripts, CSVs and crops are in `references/lighting/`. No stills were rendered and
nothing under `your Remotion project` was edited. The recipe values are starting points: they come from a numerical model of our
render path (`scripts/model.py`), and the calibration loop in 9.8 tunes them on the first real render.

## 0. The gap in one table

| What | Reference (measured) | Ours (measured) | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Day shadow on the same surface, shade/lit | Y **0.49-0.52** on wood (0.37-0.65 overall). Per channel R .54-.56, G .46-.50, B .47-.50: slightly redder and **more** saturated (sat +.02-.13) | Y **0.25-0.38**. Deck B ratio .11-.18 (muddy orange). Paving goes grey (sat .17 → .07, hue 40° → 176°) | Our shadows are ~2x too dark, lose their colour and turn grey/blue |
| Sunset shadow, shade/lit | Y **0.15-0.27**. Per channel R .08-.15, G .16-.30, **B 1.35-3.04**: violet-blue shadow on orange ground (#db9044 → #5b5173). Not possible with additive light, so it is a stylised shadow colour | Golden ground is V .30-.47, sat .74-.95 dark olive/brown everywhere, with no light/shadow pattern. Model: ground shade/lit ≈ (.55, .64, .81) | We have no golden shadows at all |
| Key direction vs camera | Day: 6/20 frames side-back from screen right (az ~50-70°), 7 frontal-soft (facade shots), 2 top. Sunset: **10/15 backlit** (sun ahead, az 0-40°, el ~3-15°), long shadows toward the camera | 29 of 41 camera keys **front-lit** (sun 116-163° from the view axis), 9 side, 3 back. All 6 golden keys 114-156°: **no backlight** | Flat front light by day; golden is lit from behind the camera |
| In-focus shadow edges (10-90 %, px @720) | 3-12 px (d03 sun patch 7-12, blobs 3-12). Foreground shadows look 16-124 px soft only because of **DOF** | 3-10 px | Same range. Edge softness is not a lighting gap; DOF is (depth.md) |
| Contact / AO | No SSAO-like darkening. Contact = ink line + cast/blob shadow. Small props get a short ramp (bowl: 25 % darker, gone in 8 px) | None | Nothing to add except blobs under characters |
| Character ground shadow | Day: soft **blob** offset away from the sun, Y ratio .52-.74, edge 3-12 px. Sunset: long soft figure shadows | Hard full-silhouette shadows of the cards (rifles, legs) in every light | Need blobs (day) and soft long shadows (golden) |
| Heads | V **.97** (.86-1.0) in every light; sat .03 day → .23 sunset → .21 night (tinted by the scene); side gradient \|lr\| **.14** (.05-.36) toward the key; top brighter (tb −.11); rim 1.03 (none) | V .82 (.65-.92). Shade heads grey #ae9786 (V .68). Golden heads #bd7c48 / #a46d41 (sat .61-.62, V .64-.74). Gradient .008 (flat) | Ours go grey in shade and orange-dark at golden, and are flat |
| Sky in frame (day top) | #238bf4 / #0a6beb / #377ff1 / #5ca3e0 (sat .59-.96) over a pale horizon (#b5d9fb-#f1e3d4) | #8bb5e6 top, #9abfe9 low (sat .34-.40, no gradient) | Washed out. Fix in depth.md 6.5 |
| Grade (day) | L* p2 15.5, p98 95.3, vivid .28, dark hue 39° C 12 (warm darks) | L* p2 1.2, p98 79.6, vivid .05, dark C 4 (grey darks) | Darks crushed and grey, highlights dull |
| Specular | Only on metal: copper pot (d01), cannon (d12), armour (d15), iron ball (d20) | none (all Lambert) | Iron and steel could get one highlight |

## 1. Method (short)

- **Same-surface pairs.**
  - Lit and shade patches are taken on one material: hand boxes (M, P rows) or an Otsu 2-cluster split of log-luminance inside a region (R, Q rows).
  - Values are averaged in linear light. Ratios are per channel, linear.
  - Scripts: `scripts/run_pairs.py`, `scripts/run_auto.py`. Checks: `crops/pairs_check.jpg`, `crops/pairs_auto_check.jpg`.
- **Edges.**
  - A logistic fit (soft-L1) across a shadow edge; 10-90 % width = 4.394·σ.
  - Script: `scripts/edges.py`, plus the extra fits in section 4. Check: `crops/edges_check.jpg`.
- **Heads.**
  - Pale round blobs inside a dark ring (`scripts/heads.py`, `scripts/heads_ours.py`).
  - 30 reference heads were checked by eye; the false hits (hats, sails, snow, paper) were dropped.
  - A plane fit of linear luminance gives grad_lr and grad_tb: the change across the head in fractions of its mean.
  - Checks: `crops/heads_check_0.jpg`, `crops/heads_ours_check.jpg`.
- **Glows.** Radial median profile around each source (`scripts/glows.py`, `crops/glows_check.jpg`).
- **Grade.** Lab percentiles, plus the hue and chroma of the dark, mid and bright bands (`scripts/grade.py`).
- **Contact / AO.** Profiles from inside an object out across its base (`scripts/contact.py`).
- **Our sun vs camera.** Each camera key in `story.ts` is parsed against `LIGHTS` (`sun_vs_cam.txt`).
- **Model.** `scripts/model.py`: three r175 Lambert + hemisphere + directional, then PBR-Neutral tone map, then sRGB.
  - It reproduces our measured shadows: slab predicted #687175 vs measured #616868; rail-post deck #52391f vs #5a3b12.
  - It is then used to solve the new values.

| **Q02** | **b_100** | **our deck, soldier band (day)** | #b37736 | #794913 | .42 | .36 | .18 | .38 | 31 → 32 | .70 → .84 |
| **P01** | **b_1810** | **our paving, sign shadow (day)** | #ccc0a9 | #616868 | .20 | .26 | .35 | .25 | 40 → 176 | .17 → .07 |
| **P02** | **b_2010** | **our river, bridge shadow** | #3f7db7 | #0f4b87 | .09 | .34 | .51 | .36 | 209 → 210 | .65 → .89 |

**Why ours look like this** (model check).
- Our shade is hemisphere + fill only: physically (0.28, 0.36, 0.50) of the lit value.
- The PBR-Neutral toe then subtracts `x − 6.25x²` from every channel whose minimum is below 0.08. That crushes the small blue channel of warm shadows (deck B ratio .11-.18) and greys out the stone.
- In golden, the 14.5° sun adds little to horizontal ground, so the ground is shade-level everywhere.

**Rule taken from the reference.**
- **Day.** Shadow = about 0.5 × lit, keeping the surface's own hue with a little more saturation (warm on wood, green on grass, bluish only on grey metal).
- **Sunset.** Shadow = a violet-blue replacement colour (blue up to 3x the lit blue).
- **Interiors.** Light patches are colour replacements too (yellow patch on a green wall).
- Ratios like these need a shader that mixes a lit colour and a shadow colour. Light intensities alone cannot produce them.

## 4. Shadow edges, contact and AO

**Penumbra, 10-90 % width in px @720.**

| ID | Frame | Edge | Width | In focus? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| new | d03 | sun patch through rigging, top / right edge | **7.1 / 12.5** | yes |
| E05 | d13 | blob under character | **9.2** | yes |
| E06 / new | d20 | soldier blob (side / lower edge) | **11.5 / 2.8** | yes |
| new | d14 | chest shadow, side edge | **5.0** | yes |
| E01-E03 | s01-s03 | long sunset shadows, near the bottom of frame | 15.8 / 20.2 / 57.4 | no, foreground DOF |
| E10 | s15 | crate shadow, foreground | 39.0 | no, DOF |
| E11 | s11 | figure shadow on floor strip | 69.2 | no, DOF |
| E12 | s04 | figures' shadow on sand | 123.8 | no, DOF |
| E09 | i02 | sun-shaft edge (dusty air) | 48.1 | volumetric |
| O01 | ours b_1900 | rail-post shadow on deck | **10.0** | – |
| O02 | ours b_1810 | sign shadow on paving | **2.9** | – |

Excluded: E04 (box inside the patch), E07, E08, O03, O04 (fit failed: plank lines, water).

**Result.**
- In-focus reference shadows are crisp, at 0.4-1.7 % of frame width. That is our range too.
- The very soft shadows in the reference all sit in DOF-blurred foreground.
- So keep a crisp shadow map. The softness comes from depth.md's lens blur, not from a big light.

**Contact darkening / AO** (`contact.csv`; distances in px @720 after the ink line).

| ID | Frame | Contact | Darkening next to base | Back to 90 % at |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C01 | i01 | bowl on counter | **25 %** | 8 px |
| – | d11 | boardwalk front → sand | black gap under boards, then a soft ramp | 11 px |
| C03 | d14 | chest base on deck | ink line only | 3 px |
| C04 | d16 | box base on ground | none | 2 px |
| – | d12 | bucket base on deck | none (plateau right after the ink) | 0 |
| C02 | i12 | under counter-top lip | none (face slightly brighter) | 1 px |
| – | i10 | wall toward floor | 15 % gradient over 110 px (lighting, not AO) | – |

**Result.**
- The reference has no screen-space-AO look. Contact is the ink line plus cast or blob shadows.
- Only small props get a short 8-11 px ramp, and that is their cast shadow.
- Our stills have no AO either. Do not add SSAO.

**Character ground shadows** (`crops/sbs_contact.jpg`).

| | Reference | Ours |
|---|---|---|
| Day | Soft elliptical **blob** offset away from the sun (d13, d20, d09). Y ratio .52-.74 (R04 .52, R06 .65, E05 .63, E06 .74). Edge 3-12 px | Full card silhouettes with rifles and legs. Hard, 3-10 px |
| Sunset | Long soft figure silhouettes (s04, s11), DOF-softened | Same hard silhouettes |
| Interiors | Often no shadow at all (i14 forge, d17 grating) | – |

**Measured head colours** (white albedo, so they read as the light colour).
- Day: #f8f1ef, #f7f1f0.
- Sunset: #fddeb6, #fde7d0.
- Warm lamp: #fcdfbd, #fbdeb5.
- Moonlight: #b1ace7, #edd9f7.
- Window light: #d9b5e7. In i08 a bright window stripe crosses the face.

**What the reference does with the cards.**
- Heads stay bright (never below V .86).
- They take the scene light colour as a tint.
- They have a soft side gradient toward the key (~14 % across the head) and a slightly brighter top.
- They receive shadows and light patterns (i08, i09) but only dim a little.
- There is no rim line.
- characters.md adds a soft crescent on the shadow side (0.88-0.89x, lavender ×(0.92, 0.90, 0.96)) and a stronger torso gradient (1.0 → 0.55-0.66).

**Ours.**
- Flat. The pasted-on look comes from emissive 0.36.
- Grey in shade (#ae9786).
- Over-orange and dark in golden (#bd7c48, #a46d41).

- **Specular.** Only metal props have it, as one soft broad highlight. Wood, ground and characters have none.
- **Rim / back light.** None on heads (rim 1.02-1.04 in both). Sunset backlight shows as bloom and haze around silhouettes, not as rim lines.
- **Haze.**
  - Day distance is blue: hills #94cefe, cliffs lavender #a7a3af.
  - Sunset distance is pink and peach: castle #db9594, palace #d1a57c.
  - Night fog is violet #877aea.
  - Exact sky and fog numbers are in depth.md 2.2/6.5; my in-frame top-of-sky samples agree with them (`sky.txt`).

## 9. Recipe for our stack (three 0.175, postprocessing 6.37.2)

The idea:
- Every world material (Lambert from `lam()` / `toLambert()`) gets a stylised **two-tone** shading: `albedo × mix(shadeCol, litCol, sunTerm)`.
  - `sunTerm` = the real shadow-map visibility × a soft N·L terminator.
  - The shadow colour is chosen per preset from the measured ratios.
- The character cards get their own light-colour tint with a bright floor and a side gradient. No emissive.
- The sun is placed **relative to each shot's camera** and frozen for the whole shot.

Shadow-map visibility is captured inside three's own light loop, so nothing is computed twice.

### 9.1 `lights.ts`: full replacement

The sky and fog fields are depth.md's values, so both recipes merge into one table.

```ts
// Light presets per time of day. The sun is CAMERA-RELATIVE and frozen per shot (world.ts update()):
// az = degrees from the shot camera's forward axis (0 = sun straight ahead = backlight, +90 = screen right,
// 180 = behind camera), el = elevation. lit/shade/lift are LINEAR RGB multipliers for shade.ts (may exceed 1).
type C3 = [number, number, number];
export type LightPreset = {
  az: number; el: number;
  lit: C3; shade: C3; lift: C3; lambert: number; sideK: number; term: number;      // world two-tone (shade.ts)
  charTint: C3; charShade: number; charGrad: number; bodyGrad: number; sideTint: C3; // character cards (cast.ts)
  blob: C3; actorShadow: boolean;                                                    // contact blobs / card shadows
  top: string; hor: string; skyCurve: C3; fog: string; fogNear: number; fogFar: number; sunDisc: string; // depth.md 6.5
  sunCol: string; sunI: number; sky: string; ground: string; hemiI: number;         // three lights (unpatched mats only)
  bias: number; normalBias: number;
};
export const LIGHTS: Record<"day" | "golden", LightPreset> = {
  day: {
    az: 75, el: 48,
    lit: [1.54, 1.47, 1.35], shade: [0.80, 0.71, 0.83], lift: [0, 0, 0], lambert: 0.35, sideK: 0.82, term: 0.2,
    charTint: [1.0, 0.94, 0.92], charShade: 0.86, charGrad: 0.07, bodyGrad: 0.2, sideTint: [0.92, 0.90, 0.96],
    blob: [0.62, 0.58, 0.58], actorShadow: false,
    top: "#4284d3", hor: "#c9cdee", skyCurve: [0.0, 0.5, 0.6], fog: "#b0c0e8", fogNear: 25, fogFar: 420, sunDisc: "#000000",
    sunCol: "#fff3e0", sunI: 2.3, sky: "#dcecff", ground: "#b49a78", hemiI: 0.9,
    bias: -0.00025, normalBias: 0.015,
  },
  golden: {
    az: 15, el: 9,
    lit: [1.42, 0.79, 0.48], shade: [0.176, 0.206, 0.72], lift: [0.035, 0.03, 0.075], lambert: 0.35, sideK: 0.82, term: 0.07,
    charTint: [1.0, 0.78, 0.56], charShade: 0.88, charGrad: 0.1, bodyGrad: 0.25, sideTint: [0.85, 0.80, 0.95],
    blob: [0.75, 0.74, 0.9], actorShadow: true,
    top: "#443876", hor: "#ffd08a", skyCurve: [0.05, 0.6, 1.0], fog: "#f4c68c", fogNear: 20, fogFar: 360, sunDisc: "#ffd9a0",
    sunCol: "#ffab5e", sunI: 5.5, sky: "#b9a4e0", ground: "#7a5a6a", hemiI: 0.5,
    bias: -0.0006, normalBias: 0.03,
  },
};
```

Predicted results (`scripts/model.py`, section 5 of its output; up-facing ground; then the camera-facing wall). These
include the Neutral tone map:

| Material | Day lit | Day shade | Day Y ratio | Golden lit | Golden shade | Golden Y ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| wood (deck) | #db9349 | #a76832 | .50 (ref #c08e5d/#936240) | #bd6010 | #45232e | .14 |
| sand | #f2d291 | #c7a57b | .60 | **#dc9244** | **#565072** | .25 (ref #db9044 / #5b5173) |
| stone / paving | #efdfbf | #bfaba3 | .57 | #d49864 | #515598 | .28 |
| grass | #82c44f | #5d8f37 | .50 (ref #6ca937/#518c27) | #708213 | #14483a | .26 |
| brick wall in shade | – | #973114 | – | – | #450620 + lift → plum | – |

**Notes on the presets.**
- **Per-shot override.** Allow per-shot `sunAz?` / `sunEl?` in `SHOTS` (story.ts) when a facade must be front-lit. Example: shot 2 town wide, az 120.
- **Golden backlight.** az 15 / el 9 puts the sun just outside the right frame edge for every golden camera. Its halo enters the frame, and 2 m cards cast ~13 m shadows toward the camera, as in s01-s04.
- **Head tints.** `charTint` values are the measured light colours for WHITE heads, which is what the reference uses. If our heads keep the peach skin #f7d6b6, use golden `[1.0, 0.88, 0.76]` and day `[1.0, 0.97, 0.96]` so head saturation stays ≤ .37 instead of .46.

### 9.2 New file `src/videos/bridge/shade.ts`: two-tone world shading + card shading

```ts
// Stylised two-tone lighting shared by all world materials and the character cards (three r175 chunk names).
import * as THREE from "three";

export const TONE = {
  uLitCol: { value: new THREE.Vector3(1.54, 1.47, 1.35) }, uShadeCol: { value: new THREE.Vector3(0.8, 0.71, 0.83) },
  uShadeLift: { value: new THREE.Vector3() }, uLambert: { value: 0.35 }, uSideK: { value: 0.82 }, uTerm: { value: 0.2 },
};
export const CHAR = {
  uCharTint: { value: new THREE.Vector3(1, 0.94, 0.92) }, uCharShade: { value: 0.86 }, uGrad: { value: 0.07 },
  uBodyGrad: { value: 0.2 }, uSideTint: { value: new THREE.Vector3(0.92, 0.9, 0.96) },
};

// Capture the sun's shadow visibility inside three's light loop (directional light 0 = the only shadow caster;
// three sorts shadow-casting lights first). Only the UNROLLED index 0 compiles the shadow block.
export const LFB = THREE.ShaderChunk.lights_fragment_begin
  .replace("directLight.color *= ( directLight.visible && receiveShadow ) ? getShadow( directionalShadowMap[ i ]",
           "sunVis = ( directLight.visible && receiveShadow ) ? getShadow( directionalShadowMap[ i ]")
  .replace("vDirectionalShadowCoord[ i ] ) : 1.0;", "vDirectionalShadowCoord[ i ] ) : 1.0;\n\t\tdirectLight.color *= sunVis;");
if (!LFB.includes("directLight.color *= sunVis")) throw new Error("shade.ts: three lights_fragment_begin changed");

const TWO_TONE = /* glsl */ `
#if NUM_DIR_LIGHTS > 0
{
  vec3 sunV = directionalLights[ 0 ].direction;                               // view space, towards the sun
  vec3 upV = normalize( ( viewMatrix * vec4( 0.0, 1.0, 0.0, 0.0 ) ).xyz );
  float ndl = max( dot( geometryNormal, sunV ), 0.0 );
  float sunTerm = sunVis * smoothstep( 0.0, uTerm, ndl );                     // 0 = cast/form shadow, 1 = lit
  float skyK = mix( uSideK, 1.0, 0.5 + 0.5 * dot( geometryNormal, upV ) );    // up-facing a bit brighter
  vec3 lit = uLitCol * mix( 1.0, ndl, uLambert );                             // keep some cosine modelling
  reflectedLight.directDiffuse = diffuseColor.rgb * mix( uShadeCol, lit, sunTerm ) * skyK + uShadeLift * ( 1.0 - sunTerm );
  reflectedLight.indirectDiffuse = vec3( 0.0 );                               // hemisphere/fill no longer used here
}
#endif
`;
const DECL = "#include <common>\nuniform vec3 uLitCol; uniform vec3 uShadeCol; uniform vec3 uShadeLift; uniform float uLambert; uniform float uSideK; uniform float uTerm;";

/** Lambert (or Phong: its specular term is kept) -> two-tone. Call once per material, before first render. */
export function twoTone<T extends THREE.MeshLambertMaterial | THREE.MeshPhongMaterial>(m: T): T {
  m.onBeforeCompile = (sh) => {
    Object.assign(sh.uniforms, TONE);
    sh.fragmentShader = sh.fragmentShader
      .replace("#include <common>", DECL)
      .replace("#include <lights_fragment_begin>", "float sunVis = 1.0;\n" + LFB)
      .replace("#include <lights_fragment_end>", "#include <lights_fragment_end>\n" + TWO_TONE);
  };
  m.customProgramCacheKey = () => "twoTone1";
  return m;
}

// Character card: bright floor, scene tint, side gradient toward the sun (head soft, torso strong), lavender
// shadow side; receives shadows but only dims to uCharShade. Replaces the whole outgoing light (no emissive).
const CARD = /* glsl */ `
vec3 outgoingLight = diffuseColor.rgb * uCharTint;
#if NUM_DIR_LIGHTS > 0
{
  vec3 sunV = directionalLights[ 0 ].direction;
  vec3 upV = normalize( ( viewMatrix * vec4( 0.0, 1.0, 0.0, 0.0 ) ).xyz );
  float side = clamp( sunV.x * 1.4, -1.0, 1.0 );                                       // + = sun on screen right
  float u = clamp( ( vMapUv.x - 0.5 ) / 0.21, -1.0, 1.0 ) * sign( dFdx( vMapUv.x ) + 1e-7 ); // -1..1 left..right
  float t = u * side;                                                                    // +1 = edge facing the sun
  float head = smoothstep( 0.55, 0.62, vMapUv.y );                                       // head zone vs torso
  float g = mix( uBodyGrad, uGrad, head );
  float dark = smoothstep( 0.1, 1.0, -t );                                               // crescent on the far side
  vec3 sideK = mix( vec3( 1.0 ), uSideTint, dark * ( g / max( uGrad, 1e-3 ) ) * 0.5 );
  float top = 1.0 + 0.055 * clamp( ( vMapUv.y - 0.72 ) / 0.15, -1.0, 1.0 ) * clamp( dot( sunV, upV ) * 2.0, 0.0, 1.0 ) * head;
  float lift = 1.0 + 0.03 * max( t, 0.0 );
  outgoingLight *= mix( uCharShade, 1.0, sunVis ) * ( 1.0 - g * dark ) * sideK * top * lift;
}
#endif
`;
export function cardShade(m: THREE.MeshLambertMaterial) {
  m.emissive.set(0x000000); m.emissiveIntensity = 0; m.emissiveMap = null;
  m.onBeforeCompile = (sh) => {
    Object.assign(sh.uniforms, CHAR);
    sh.fragmentShader = sh.fragmentShader
      .replace("#include <common>", "#include <common>\nuniform vec3 uCharTint; uniform float uCharShade; uniform float uGrad; uniform float uBodyGrad; uniform vec3 uSideTint;")
      .replace("#include <lights_fragment_begin>", "float sunVis = 1.0;\n" + LFB)
      .replace("vec3 outgoingLight = reflectedLight.directDiffuse + reflectedLight.indirectDiffuse + totalEmissiveRadiance;", CARD);
  };
  m.customProgramCacheKey = () => "card1";
  return m;
}
```

What the card shader gives, using the model with our skin #f7d6b6:
- **Day.** Lit head #ecc6a4 (V .93). In shadow #e2bb9c (V .89), against today's #ae9786 (V .68).
- **Gradient.** Head about −14 % at the far edge (matches the .14 median); torso about −20 to −25 %, close to characters.md's 0.55-0.66 at the far edge with `bodyGrad` .2-.25.
- **Head zone.** The 0.55-0.62 v split and the 0.72 head centre assume the 600x1000 card with the head in the top third. Adjust them if characters.md changes the proportions.

### 9.3 `build.ts`: apply it everywhere the world gets a material

- `lam()`: `m = twoTone(new THREE.MeshLambertMaterial(make()))`.
- `toLambert()`: wrap the new Lambert the same way: `twoTone(new THREE.MeshLambertMaterial({...}))`.
- **Metal highlights** (the reference shows one soft highlight on metal only):
  - In `world.ts` use `M.iron = twoTone(new THREE.MeshPhongMaterial({ color: "#2c2f36", specular: "#8a8a8a", shininess: 60 }))` and `M.steel = twoTone(new THREE.MeshPhongMaterial({ color: "#9aa3b3", specular: "#b0b0b0", shininess: 45 }))`.
  - Phong keeps `directSpecular`, which is masked by the shadow because the sun colour was multiplied by `sunVis`.
- The water `MeshLambertMaterial` in world.ts should also go through `twoTone()`, so the bridge shadow on the river takes the shade colour.

### 9.4 `world.ts`: rig, sun per shot, uniforms, blobs

In the constructor:

```ts
import { TONE, CHAR, twoTone } from "./shade";
// sun disc direction for depth.md's sky/sun sprite: keep a vector
private sunDir = new THREE.Vector3();
// contact blobs: one instanced multiply-decal, drawn after ground (renderOrder 0.5) and before cards (renderOrder 1)
private blobs = new THREE.InstancedMesh(new THREE.PlaneGeometry(1, 1), new THREE.ShaderMaterial({
  uniforms: { uRatio: { value: new THREE.Color(0.62, 0.58, 0.58) } },
  vertexShader: "varying vec2 vUv; void main(){ vUv = uv; gl_Position = projectionMatrix * modelViewMatrix * instanceMatrix * vec4(position, 1.0); }",
  fragmentShader: "uniform vec3 uRatio; varying vec2 vUv; void main(){ float r = length(vUv * 2.0 - 1.0); float m = 1.0 - smoothstep(0.72, 1.0, r); gl_FragColor = vec4(mix(vec3(1.0), uRatio, m), 1.0); }",
  transparent: false, depthWrite: false, blending: THREE.CustomBlending, blendEquation: THREE.AddEquation,
  blendSrc: THREE.ZeroFactor, blendDst: THREE.SrcColorFactor, polygonOffset: true, polygonOffsetFactor: -2, polygonOffsetUnits: -2,
}), 160);
// ...in the constructor body: this.blobs.renderOrder = 0.5; this.blobs.frustumCulled = false; R.add(this.blobs);
// and give every Actor mesh renderOrder = 1 (cast.ts), so cards are drawn over the multiplied ground.
```

In `update()`, replace the "light, sky, fog" block:

```ts
const L = LIGHTS[s.light];
// camera-relative sun, frozen per shot: use the camera at the shot's first frame
const c0 = camAt(id, s.a);
let fx = c0.look[0] - c0.pos[0], fz = c0.look[2] - c0.pos[2]; const fn = Math.hypot(fx, fz) || 1; fx /= fn; fz /= fn;
const az = THREE.MathUtils.degToRad(s.sunAz ?? L.az), el = THREE.MathUtils.degToRad(s.sunEl ?? L.el);
const hx = fx * Math.cos(az) - fz * Math.sin(az), hz = fz * Math.cos(az) + fx * Math.sin(az); // screen right = (-fz, fx)
const sunDir = this.sunDir.set(hx * Math.cos(el), Math.sin(el), hz * Math.cos(el));
this.sun.color.set(L.sunCol); this.sun.intensity = L.sunI;
this.hemi.color.set(L.sky); this.hemi.groundColor.set(L.ground); this.hemi.intensity = L.hemiI;
this.fill.intensity = 0;                                    // not used by the two-tone shading
const Rr = THREE.MathUtils.clamp(dist * 1.15 + 7, 12, 95);
this.sun.position.copy(focus).addScaledVector(sunDir, 220); this.sun.target.position.copy(focus);
// (shadow camera as today)
this.sun.shadow.bias = L.bias; this.sun.shadow.normalBias = L.normalBias * (Rr / 12);
TONE.uLitCol.value.set(...L.lit); TONE.uShadeCol.value.set(...L.shade); TONE.uShadeLift.value.set(...L.lift);
TONE.uLambert.value = L.lambert; TONE.uSideK.value = L.sideK; TONE.uTerm.value = L.term;
CHAR.uCharTint.value.set(...L.charTint); CHAR.uCharShade.value = L.charShade; CHAR.uGrad.value = L.charGrad;
CHAR.uBodyGrad.value = L.bodyGrad; CHAR.uSideTint.value.set(...L.sideTint);
(this.blobs.material as THREE.ShaderMaterial).uniforms.uRatio.value.setRGB(...L.blob);
// sky + fog: depth.md 6.5 (top/hor/skyCurve/fog/fogNear/fogFar); sun disc sprite (depth.md 6.4) at cam + sunDir*900, colour L.sunDisc
```

In the actor loop:
- Delete `a.mat.emissive.set(tint)`.
- Add `a.mesh.castShadow = L.actorShadow;`.
- Add one blob per standing card (skip `crowd: "water"` shots and swing riders):

```ts
const hl = Math.hypot(sunDir.x, sunDir.z) || 1, ox = -sunDir.x / hl, oz = -sunDir.z / hl;   // away from the sun
this.e.set(-Math.PI / 2, Math.atan2(-ox, -oz), 0, "YXZ");                                     // long axis along shadow
this.m4.compose(this.v.set(p.x + ox * 0.25 * sc2, p.y + 0.012, p.z + oz * 0.25 * sc2), this.q.setFromEuler(this.e),
                this.s3.set(0.7 * sc2, 1.1 * sc2, 1));
this.blobs.setMatrixAt(bi++, this.m4);
// after the loop: this.blobs.count = bi; this.blobs.instanceMatrix.needsUpdate = true;
```

Blob values: an ellipse 0.7 × 1.1 m offset 0.25 m away from the sun. Its multiply is `L.blob` (day Y ≈ .59 = the
reference .52-.74). The soft rim is the outer 28 % of the radius, ≈ 0.1 m, which is ~10-14 px @720 in a medium shot (reference 3-12).

**Day vs golden card shadows.**
- In day the cards do not cast: with az 75 the sun is only 15° off the card plane, so the card would cast a thin sliver of a shadow. Reference day frames show blobs instead.
- In golden they cast long silhouettes plus a light contact blob, as in s01-s04.
- This refines characters.md's "keep the cast shadow + blob".

### 9.5 `cast.ts`: Actor material

```ts
import { cardShade } from "./shade";
this.mat = cardShade(new THREE.MeshLambertMaterial({ map: blank, alphaTest: 0.5, side: THREE.DoubleSide }));
this.mat.alphaToCoverage = true;
this.mesh.receiveShadow = true;           // keeps sunVis (dims to 0.86-0.88 only)
this.mesh.renderOrder = 1;                // drawn after the contact blobs
bind() { const t = this.r?.tex; if (t) { this.mat.map = t; this.dmat.map = t; } }   // no emissiveMap any more
```

The `customDepthMaterial` (RGBADepthPacking, alphaTest 0.5) stays as it is. It also works with VSM if that is ever turned on.

### 9.6 Shadow-map settings

- **Keep `shadows` (PCFSoftShadowMap).** mapSize 4096 and the existing focus-fitted frustum `Rr = clamp(dist·1.15 + 7, 12, 95)`.
  - Texel = 2Rr/4096 (6 mm close-up, 4.6 cm wide).
  - In-focus edges stay 3-10 px @720, the same as the reference's 3-12. Do not add PCSS or big blur radii; DOF supplies the foreground softness.
- **Bias.**
  - Day: `bias −0.00025`, `normalBias 0.015·Rr/12` (as today).
  - Golden, with the sun at 9°: `bias −0.0006`, `normalBias 0.03·Rr/12` against acne on the grazing ground.
  - With two-tone colours, acne shows as violet speckles, so check the swing shot first.
- **Shadow reach.** At el 9° a 2 m card throws a ~13 m shadow, so keep `Rr ≥ 14` in golden shots. The existing clamp of 12 is just enough near the camera; use `Math.max(Rr, 16)` when `s.light === "golden"`.
- **Optional, only if edges still look too crisp after DOF.** `<ThreeCanvas shadows="variance">` with `sun.shadow.radius = 3`, `sun.shadow.blurSamples = 12`. It costs a blur pass; light bleed is fine at these ratios.

### 9.7 AO

**Do not add SSAOEffect.**
- The reference has no AO darkening (section 4).
- In postprocessing 6.37 it needs a `NormalPass`: a second full scene render that ignores our alpha-tested cards. It would add grime the reference does not have.
- If it is ever wanted for interiors:

```ts
new SSAOEffect(camera, normalPass.texture, { worldDistanceThreshold: 1, worldDistanceFalloff: 0.3,
  worldProximityThreshold: 0.4, worldProximityFalloff: 0.1, intensity: 1.2, radius: 0.05, samples: 9, rings: 4,
  luminanceInfluence: 0.7, resolutionScale: 0.5, color: new Color("#5b4a6a") })
```

Contact comes from:
- the blobs (9.4);
- the ink line (ink report);
- the two-tone cast shadows.

### 9.8 Calibration loop (first render after implementing)

1. Render the same test stills (b_100, b_1810, b_1900, c_1450, b_1500, b_1680, c_1745).
2. Run, in `references/lighting`:
   - `python scripts/run_auto.py` (Q01/Q02) and `python scripts/run_pairs.py` (P01/P02). Boxes are in pixel coordinates, so re-pick them if the framing moved.
   - `python scripts/heads_ours.py`
   - `python scripts/grade.py`
3. Check these targets:

| Measure | Target |
|---|---|
| Day deck/paving shade/lit Y | **.45-.55**; per-channel R ≥ G ≈ B; sat not lower than lit |
| Golden ground lit | V ≥ .75, hue 25-35° |
| Golden shade | B ratio ≥ 1.3 (aim 2-3), hue 240-260° |
| Heads | V ≥ .86 everywhere, \|grad_lr\| .10-.17, grad_tb −.05 to −.13 |
| Day dark-band chroma | C ≥ 10, hue 20-60° |
| Sunset split_b | ≥ +15 |

If a shade ratio misses, scale that channel of `shade` by target/measured and repeat. The Neutral toe makes it slightly non-linear, so 2 rounds are usually enough.

## 10. Top 5 changes ranked by visual impact

| # | Change | Where | What it fixes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Two-tone shadow colours** | `shade.ts` + `twoTone()` in `lam` / `toLambert` + LIGHTS lit/shade | Every frame: day shadows go from grey-muddy Y .25-.38 to warm Y .50 with saturation kept; golden shadows become violet-blue (B ≈ 3x lit) |
| 2 | **Golden as backlight** | camera-relative sun az 15° / el 9° + depth.md sunset sky and sun disc | Long shadows toward the camera, lit ground V ≥ .75, violet shade, sun glow at the frame edge. Shots 11-13 change from "dim orange day" to the reference's signature sunset |
| 3 | **Card shading** | `cardShade()`: no emissive, tint per scene, bright floor .86, side gradient toward the sun (head ~14 %, torso ~20-25 %), lavender far side | Heads stop going grey (V .68 → .89) or orange-dark (sat .62 → ~.3); characters belong to the light |
| 4 | **Day key from the side, frozen per shot** | az 75 / el 48 | Deck and rail shadows run across the frame and facades get a lit and a shade side, instead of flat front light in 29/41 keys |
| 5 | **Character ground shadows** | Offset soft multiply blobs (Y ~.6) under every standing card; card shadow casting off by day, on at golden | Replaces the hard rifle-and-leg silhouettes; grounds the crowd like the reference blobs |

Sky and fog (depth.md 6.5) rank next to #4 but are already specified there. Metal highlights (9.3) are small.

# Motion: reference vs ours (camera grammar + character animation)

Units: **px = pixels at the reference size 720x1280** (multiply by 1.5 for our 1080x1920). **f** = frame at 30 fps.
Reference = 20 shorts, 32,337 frames, burned-in caption band excluded from every camera measurement.
Ours = `story.ts` / `cast.ts` / `toon.ts` as of 2026-10-31. Rendering was off-limits, so ours is measured **from the code with
the same maths**: `ourcam.py` runs the exact `CAMS` + `run()` + `camAt()` (a private copy, executed with node) through the same
per-frame similarity fit and the same statistics as the reference; `ourwalk.py` evaluates the `bodyFront` / `bodySide` formulas.

## 0. Verdict

1. **Our camera moves are 3-frame snaps; the reference glides.** Our key moves peak at a median 115 px/f (up to 488) with a 3 f
   attack. Reference moves are symmetric ease-in-out: ~10 f in, ~10 f out, ~23 f total, with a median peak of 14-16 px/f.
   The "ease-out burst" grammar in STYLE_BIBLE.md (and in the `story.ts` header comment) came from an integer phase
   correlation that cannot see motion below ~4 px/f. It is wrong.
2. **The reference camera is almost never dead still.** 65 % of frames move. Even in quiet frames it keeps pushing in
   (0.14 %/f median). There is no hand-held float at all. Impacts are 6-7 frame kicks that flip sign every frame.
3. **Characters are animated on ones.** No line boil, no squash/stretch, no smears, no idle loops. Everything happens on
   acting beats that land 0-3 f before the word.
4. **Walks are bouncy and planted.**
   - Body bob is 3-7 % of height; ours is 1.7 % in front view.
   - Lean swings from 1° to 7° every step.
   - A step takes 9-12 f; ours takes 15 f, and 20-38 f for the tourists.
   - The stance foot sticks to the ground; ours skate 13-60 % in side views.
5. **Blinks are frequent and shaped:** every 0.5-2.3 s, 4 frames (half / shut / shut / half), plus one on every expression
   swap. Ours blink every 4 s and only open/shut.

## 1. Method (scripts in `references/motion/scripts/`)

- **Camera (`cam2.py`, `camstats.py`, `camshape.py`)**
  - Each frame is fitted against the previous one: a robust 9-tile sub-pixel phase-correlation similarity fit (translation,
    zoom, roll) at 360x640. Tile bands are 3-29 %, 29-56 % and 80-97 % of the height, so captions are excluded. Outlier tiles
    are rejected.
  - The fits are integrated into a path and smoothed with a Gaussian of σ = 1.2 f.
  - Speed is translation combined with zoom × 734 px (the frame-corner radius).
  - Classes: hold < 0.3 px/f, drift < 2, move ≥ 2.
  - Bursts are local peaks ≥ 6 px/f. Attack and release are measured to 10 % of the peak.
- **Ours (`ours/dump.mjs`, `ourcam.py`)**
  - `camAt()` is projected through a 5x5 point grid on the subject plane into the next frame, then fitted the same way.
  - Shot boundaries with no on-screen cut (shots 3-8 and 10 start from the previous framing) are kept as moves.
  - Real cuts (flash, crossfades) are masked like the reference cuts.
- **Characters**
  - Contact sheets of 2-4 s at 30 fps. 
  - NCC template tracking (`track.py`), compensated for camera motion with background patches (`idle.py`, `walkstat.py`).
  - Frame-diff hold detection (`uniq.py`).
  - Eye-strip dark-pixel counts (`blink.py`).
  - Mouth blob area vs the voice RMS envelope, tested at lags −4..+4 f (`mouth.py`).

## 2. Camera

### 2.1 Reference vs ours

| Measure | Reference (median, range over 20 videos) | Ours (computed from story.ts) | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hold (< 0.3 px/f) | 10 % (1-27 %) | 22 % | more dead-still frames |
| Drift (0.3-2 px/f) | 24 % (4-38 %) | 22 % | ≈ |
| Move (≥ 2 px/f) | 65 % (34-94 %) | 55 % | ≈ |
| Median speed | 3.6 px/f (1.3-12.9) | 2.9 px/f | ≈ |
| p90 speed | 16.9 px/f (9.7-29.4) | 28.1 px/f | ours has spikes |
| Moves per 10 s | 6.0 (2.9-8) | 2.2 | half as many |
| Move peak speed | 13.7 px/f (p25 5.9, p75 24.6, p90 36) | **115 px/f** (79-488 on the big ones) | **8x too fast** |
| Burst attack (10 % to peak) | 10 f (p25 6, p75 18) | **3 f** | snap |
| Burst release (peak to 10 %) | 10 f (p25 6, p75 19) | 6 f | |
| Attack / release | 1.0; 62 % symmetric, 18 % ease-out, 20 % ease-in | 0.57; quartic ease-out puts 26-41 % of the move in frame 1 (dur 14-8) | wrong curve |
| Speed at ±10 f from the peak (median profile) | 0.6 of peak (full width at half max > 20 f) | 0.03-0.05 of peak (full width at half max ≈ 5 f) | see the profile plot |
| Size of one move | 101 px, zoom 23 % | 331 px, zoom 46 % | 3x bigger moves in 1/4 of the time |
| Strong moves (peak ≥ 25 px/f) | attack 8 f, release 7 f; e.g. v14 160-240: 1→38 px/f in 16 f, 12 f plateau, 13 f release | 3 f attack, 5-7 f release | |
| Zoom in quiet frames (translation < 2 px/f) | active (≥ 0.05 %/f) in 70 % of frames, 83 % of those push in; rate median 0.14 %/f, p75 0.34, p90 0.70 | active 55 %, 63 % push in; median 0.06 %/f, p75 0.16 | pushes 2.4x slower, often lateral drift instead |
| Push-in runs (≥ 12 f) | 336 push-ins vs 180 pull-outs; median 35 f, +22 %, 0.61 %/f. Slow ones (< 0.3 %/f): 25 f, +3.7 % | linear `drift()` | no deliberate push-ins |
| Snap zoom / crane | v08 snap zoom 0.4→5.9 %/f (+38 % in 14 f); v03 crane pull-back up to −4.6 %/f, ~45 f speeding up, ~60 f slowing down | 13 moves that zoom by up to 275 % or travel 0.7-3.7 frame widths in 3-8 f | |
| Roll moves | 3.2 per 10 s, median 3.6° over 18 f (p75 8.1°, p90 17.5°) | 3.0 per 10 s, ~2.5° over ~9 f (max 9.6°) | half as long, a bit small |
| Hand-held float | **none**: high-frequency residual 0.08 px median (0.05-0.29) | 0.006 m sine float = 0.03 px/f median (invisible) | delete it |
| Impact shake | v14 spear: offset flips sign **every frame** (15 Hz), ~3 px offset (≈5 px/f), roll ±0.1-0.35°, gone in 6-8 f; the push-in stops at the hit | 6.4 Hz sine, linear decay over 15 f: bolt 28.7 px/f, deck→water 15.5, hammer 10.7 on the first frame | 2-5x stronger, 2x longer, slow wobble |
| Whip pans | 0.45 per minute (5 real ones in 18 min). 45-93 px/f for 7-25 f, 2-3 f ramp up and down, **strong directional motion blur**: v13 175-183 (30→93→25), v07 58-90 (~47 vertical), v09 1334-1343 (peak 56) | none on purpose, but 13 un-blurred "fly" moves at 79-488 px/f | |
| Crowd beats | formations stand still while a crane or truck move supplies the motion (v03 51 s, v14 0 s and 125 s) | marching column (story), townsfolk waving and hopping nonstop | keep the march, still the extras |

## 3. Character animation

| Item | Reference (measured) | Ours (from code) | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Drawing rate | **on ones**. A moving character changes every frame; no 2s or 3s holds. Static characters are pixel-identical (mean abs diff 0.15 grey levels). | March poses quantised at 1/120 of a cycle (ones). **Tourists use `q(…, 24)` at 0.39-0.75 Hz, so each pose holds 1.7-3.2 f.** | tourists are on 2s/3s |
| Step length in time | 9-12 f per step (2.5-3.3 steps/s): v04 cowboy 10.6 f, v13 carriers ~9 f, v03 kid ~12 f | march 15 f (2.0 steps/s); tourists 20-38 f (0.8-1.5 steps/s) | march 25-65 % slower; tourists 2-3x slower |
| Body bob, peak to peak | 3-7 % of height, lowest at contact: cowboy 47 px on a 617 px figure = 7.6 % (0.32 head heights); carriers ~4 %; kid ~3 % | front march 14 units = **1.7 %**, stroll **1.0 %**. Side view (from leg geometry) 4.1 % at stomp 1.0 → 9.0 % at stomp 1.5. Lowest at contact (correct). | front view 3-4x too flat |
| Lean | 1° at passing → 5-7° forward at contact, in phase with the bob (cowboy) | side: a constant 3°; front: none | no lean cycle |
| Leg action | side walk: big stride, thigh swings about ±35-45°, and the knee rises until the thigh is nearly level at passing (high-knee strut, v04 650-651 and 661-662; visual estimate) | side: thigh 30°×stomp + 16°×stomp lift, knee 70°×stomp. Front: foot lift 60 u for the march, 27 u for the stroll. | close enough at stomp ≥ 1.35 |
| Feet | **planted**. The stance foot moves with the ground at ≤ 1.1 px/f while the ground moves 7.2 px/f (v04 649-651, tracked). A soft blue-grey contact shadow sits under the foot. | **Side views skate**: column 39 % (stomp 1.0), 20 % (1.35), 13 % (1.5); tourists ~60 %. The legs (250 u = 0.54 m) cannot cover 0.8 m steps at 1.6 m/s and 2 steps/s. | visible in shots 4, 13, 15 |
| Arms | noodle arms hang or hold props; swing too small or hidden to measure on the studied walkers | front: hand ±16 u (±4°); side: hand ±90 u (±22°) | no data, leave as is |
| Idle in holds | **none**: frozen to 0.01-0.8 px over 32 f (v20 sitting). Only acting idles, e.g. the v20 sleeper's head lolls 10-15° in 8-10 f, holds ~20 f, then returns. | standing figures frozen (good); water bob 0.03 m at 0.35 Hz (motivated, good). **Townsfolk wave at 1.4 Hz and the kid hops at 1.1 Hz without a break.** | extras too busy |
| Blinks | 3-4 f long: half, shut ×1-2 (a line), half. Gaps 0.2-2.3 s, typically 0.5-1.5 s (v19: 7 blinks in 4 s, v06: 6 in 4 s). Blinks cover expression swaps. | `blinkAt`: every 3.98 s, 4.3 f, open/shut only, not tied to swaps | 3-5x fewer, no in-between frames |
| Mouth (talking) | smooth shapes changing on ones (line, rect with teeth, open with tongue, wide); r ≈ 0.47 with the voice RMS; the mouth leads by 0-1 f (v10 officer) | no talking characters; expression mouths are static | n/a (narration only) |
| Reaction timing | expression or action change lands 0-3 f **before** the word onset (v03 −1 / −3, v10 0, v14 0). Swaps are instant (1 frame). | `exprMarch` swaps at the onset −1.6..+2.0 f (median ~0), instant | about 2 f late |
| Squash / stretch, smears | none | none | keep |
| Anticipation / impact | jump: 2-3 f crouch (v10). Spear (v14): 3 f to lower, **~6 f held back, a 1-frame thrust**, impact flash + 4-5 f ring, contact on the word onset. No overshoot frames in these sequences. | hammer: 6 f smooth raise, 2 f hold, **3 f** ease-in strike, 9 f burst; contact on the word (good) | no held wind-up, strike too slow, burst too long |
| Line boil | **none** (amplitude 0) | none | keep 0 |
| Turns | real 3D bodies (capsule + sphere); turns take 1-4 f with the face decal sliding round the head (v10). Front→back ~2 f (v16 759→761); back→front can be a 1-frame swap (v16 801→802). | yaw billboards. Front↔side swap and mirror flip happen in 1 f whenever the camera crosses cos 0.57, **including in the middle of a camera move**. | pops during glides |
